📚🔍 Aug 23 — "Oversight Doc Drift Discipline & Plan-First Restructure": DD1-DD9 shipped, and its own check immediately found 7 real stale claims across the docs it was written to protect
A ratified /CEO plan, nine items, in two halves. The structural half (DD1-DD3, DD7): ceo_plan_items gained five short Galaxy Map identifier columns (galaxy_river/galaxy_modules/galaxy_level/galaxy_dimensions/galaxy_facet_kind), every non-green item backfilled from real evidence; future_integrations.html and smoke_test.html were rebuilt Plan-first and fully live-fetched; and the Galaxy Map itself gained a live in-flight overlay, injected at the generator's own post-process point so a regeneration can't wipe it. The procedural half (DD6, DD9): a named Cross-Doc Drift Check (update-logging-system artifact 20) plus CLAUDE.md rule 16 and /CEO R24 — name the SUBJECT that changed, check only the 2-4 docs that plausibly claim something about it, grade with /drift's vocabulary, route to brown/achiever or purple/error_log. A full pairwise sweep of all 12 docs on every push is explicitly rejected as a design (rule 11).

The bug that caused all of it: future_integrations.html's A5 card asserted "Research Lab page confirmed fully intact, untouched" long after that page was retired — because it was hand-typed HTML sitting next to a live-fetched section. CLAUDE.md described the same feature accurately; the ceo_plan_items row said a third thing. Three sources of truth, silently diverged for over a week. Hence rule 16's deliberate two halves: where a doc can be rendered from real data instead of asserted by hand, do that (DD2 removed the whole class of bug rather than adding a rule to remember) — the checklist is the fallback for what genuinely can't be rendered, never the first tool reached for.

DD8 then ran that check for real, against the ten oversight docs the earlier items never touched, using this same session's own work as the fresh subject. It found nine real stale claims and fixed all nine (each logged as its own achiever_archive row, the archive's total moving 18 → 27): four docs still described the Galaxy Map drill-down chain through galaxy_map_level3.html/level4.html/l0.html/skills.html and a "Level 1.5 (Meanders)", all deleted or retired by the Aug 21 fold passes; interconnection_map.md and manual.html still described Research Lab as a live 7-tab page with a dashboard card, a drawer entry and a research:tab-active lazy-load gate; system_flow_map.md's component inventory still read "53 registered modules" (really 56 — mockOracle, errorLog and oracleProviderMode all shipped after the last re-grep) and still listed the deleted api/noter.js; three docs still quoted "16 rivers / 44 modules / 426-427 functions" (really 17 / 45 / 438); and ai_tooling_and_rules_map.md's skill catalog was missing /perspective — the second time that same list has drifted the same way, as its own note already records. A further 11 smoke_test_items rows were found citing the deleted galaxy_map_level4.html — a real miss by DD4 earlier the same day, which had swept the Galaxy Development Framework category and found five, while these eleven sat in the Dashboard Cards category, generated two weeks earlier by a different script. All eleven corrected in place (the feature each tests is unchanged; only the page to open moved), each repointed at a real, verified galaxy_map_module.html#river-N anchor.

DD8's tagging half was deliberately not uniform, per each doc's own stated role. error_log and achiever_archive gained a real galaxy_river column matching the exact ceo_plan_items/smoke_test_items precedent — same column name, same .galaxy-tag CSS class, not a third convention (rule 8); achiever_archive: 27 of 27 rows now carry a real tag (9 inserted with one already, at write time, from this pass's own findings; the 18 pre-existing rows backfilled). error_log: 2 of 3 rows tagged, with one honest blank where an error's own stack traces span two rivers with no single owner. minotaur_map.html and taxonomy_map.html were left untouched by design, not by oversight — the first updates only when a new wing is added (a cosmetic tag inside an existing corridor is not one), the second is a live taxonomy_tree query whose own rule is "only touch if its own code/columns change," and no schema change happened. manual.html's per-row tagging was tested and honestly declined with a measured number: only 11 of 51 button rows name a river-tracked module in their Chain cell, so 78% of the column would have been hand-assigned judgment — a second hand-maintained copy of a mapping that already has one real home. patch_notes.html and session_lessons.html got no retrofit either; they are dated narrative, and old cards are correct as-said.

Nine achiever_archive rows logged (brown — a stale CLAIM, not broken code; the archive's own total moved 18 → 27), each with the real old value, the real new value and a checkable reason they diverged. The recurring lesson across all of them is one thing: a writer's attention follows the file being edited, not the files that merely describe it — and a real reference can live in a Supabase row or a tooling config, not just a tracked file, which is the same lesson vercel.json, graphify_river_group.py and .claude/settings.json each taught once already. One real finding is honestly left open rather than fixed blind: DASHBOARD_CARDS in scripts/graphify_river_group.py still lists the retired "🧠 Research Lab" card, and correcting it needs a full Galaxy Map regeneration rather than a lone script edit that would leave the committed HTML disagreeing with its own generator.

Verified: headless Chromium against both rebuilt Supabase-backed docs with a mocked response — correct row counts, tags rendering only where a real river exists, zero console errors; div-balance re-checked against a fresh git show HEAD: baseline on every HTML file touched.

One real near-miss caught before it became a false finding — the dashboard's .dd-card grid's uniform gold "Enter →" styling first read as "identical undifferentiated cards," but re-checking against DESIGN.md's own existing rule ("one accent color, gold for every go action") confirmed it's the correct, deliberate execution of an already-standing rule, not a gap. Doc-only change, no application code touched.

🕵️✅ Aug 22 (immediate follow-up) — real /paranoia+Council-of-5+/interrogation on the sortEntries loose ends, plus real-world confirmation: freeze down 89%, 12-13s → 1.4s
Alex: paranoia the 2 flagged loose ends from the fix below (the case-sensitivity bug, whether to move ENC_SORT's default off 'unique'), Council of 5 the options, interrogate me, then /drift+/CEO the outcome.

Real, honest finding surfaced mid-pass: a fresh direct Supabase query found the live encyclopedia table holds only 70 real rows (median content 2435 chars, no outlier hiding a bigger real workload) — re-benchmarking the OLD algorithm at that real scale predicted only ~470ms of blocking, not 12+ seconds. The earlier "confirmed root cause" framing was ahead of the evidence. error_log was reopened rather than left standing as prematurely resolved.

Council of 5 laid out real options for both items (fix-now vs. leave-as-documented; keep-default vs. safety-valve vs. change-default vs. full algorithm rewrite) — a full rewrite was rejected as disproportionate to RPGACE's real single-user scale.

Alex's real answers: fix the case-sensitivity bug now; change the default to 'recent' now; yes, re-test live and report back.

Built: sortEntries()'s 'unique' branch now lowercases allText too (previously only the per-entry comparison text was) and excludes self by real array index instead of a string-equality check that only worked by case-matching coincidence — fixes both symptoms of the one root cause (self-inflation, missed cross-entry matches on capitalized words) in one real, Alex-approved change to the actual ranking output. ENC_SORT's default changed 'unique'→'recent'; index.html's sort buttons swapped which one carries active to match — 'unique' stays one click away.

Real-world confirmation, not just a benchmark: Alex reloaded the live deployed fix and captured a fresh Performance recording — the freeze dropped from ~12,150-13,329ms down to a single real perfWatch warning of 1,404ms (~89% reduction, a full order-of-magnitude change), confirming sortEntries really was the dominant cause. The gap between the ~235ms isolated benchmark prediction and the ~1.4s real browser figure is explained by real DOM rendering + network round trips riding along in the same measured Task — not by the fix being wrong. error_log marked resolved for real this time, backed by a live before/after measurement. A small residual ~1.4s block + 5 collapsed "setTimeout" console violations were flagged, not investigated — a separate, much smaller, lower-priority item.

/drift: Baseline RATIFIED (Alex's own 3 answers, same turn) — Work map FULL (3 items) — Verdict ON TRACK, zero gap. /CEO: deliberately did NOT get its own ceo_plans row — a small, single-session fix doesn't warrant the same ceremony as the Galaxy Map/Massive Expansion frameworks; this card + the /drift check are the right-sized record instead.

node --check clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped 20260822f→20260822g.
🐌✅ Aug 22 — the real 12+ second freeze root cause found via Alex's own DevTools flame chart: sortEntries()'s pathological default sort, 2x speedup shipped
Closes the "do this that you sent me" ask on the still-open longtask finding, for real this time. Alex hand-captured a full-duration Performance recording (after DevTools' "Record and reload" kept auto-stopping at 5-6 seconds, well before the delayed freeze — worked around with a plain Record button + manual reload + login + manual stop). His flame chart showed the real 12-13s red "Task" block under refreshEncyclopediaDisplay → renderEncEntries → sortEntries — a different root cause than either hypothesis chased earlier the same day (network volume, boot staggering), both honestly ruled out by then.

Real root cause: sortEntries()'s 'unique' mode recomputed a full cross-entry text-overlap score from scratch inside the sort comparator — the same expensive O(n) scan rerunning on every comparison instead of once. ENC_SORT's actual default value is 'unique', so this ran unconditionally on the Encyclopedia's very first render, not some rare opt-in mode.

Fixed, 100% behavior-preserving: each entry's overlap score now precomputes once into a Map before the sort runs; the comparator just reads it. Same ordering, same output, computed once instead of ~n times. A separate, pre-existing correctness bug in the same original code (a case-sensitivity inconsistency breaking self-exclusion/substring matching) was found in passing and deliberately left unfixed to keep this a pure perf fix — flagged for Alex's own future call, not silently bundled in.

Quantified before shipping, not just asserted: a standalone Node benchmark (old vs. new, both algorithms extracted verbatim) confirmed a consistent real 2.0x speedup at every size tested. At content length matching the real ~3000-char Oracle-prompt slice, the OLD algorithm's true severity showed: n=300 → 9.5s, n=500 → 27s, n=800 → 68.8s (clean O(n²) scaling — squaring the size ratio predicts the measured time almost exactly) — confirming this wasn't just "slow," it could genuinely freeze the tab for over a minute at a large enough real Encyclopedia.

Honest caveats: the benchmark uses synthetic entries — Alex's own real-world re-test is the true confirmation of how much better it feels. The 'unique' mode is still inherently O(n²)-in-entry-count even after this fix, just with the constant factor halved — whether its default should change is a real open question, not decided here.

node --check clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped 20260822e→20260822f. error_log row for the boot-lag finding updated with this final root cause.

🗂️🔀 Aug 20 (2nd pass) — main.js mechanically merged into rpgace_core.js: one client script, not two
Alex, reacting to "old vs. new" language used to describe the 2-file split: "why have 2? i want the best and newest and fastest to be the only one in use. why wasnt this flagged earlier?" Real /misunderstanding pass first: the two files were never a duplication (rule 8's own trigger) — main.js (bootstrap/routing/password gate/career score, 0 RPGACE.register() calls) and rpgace_core.js (every feature since, 60 real modules) do genuinely different work, which is exactly why rule 8's dedup scans never flagged it. Real ask, confirmed via AskUserQuestion: a MECHANICAL merge only (paste as-is, zero logic rewrite), plus a separate, larger, explicitly-deferred UI/logic split across all 60 modules (not attempted this pass).

Real evidence gathered before touching anything (rule 1): zero top-level name collisions between the two files' own declarations, but rpgace_core.js's own IIFE ((function(global){'use strict';...})(window)) meant a naive paste-inside-the-closure would have silently turned every one of main.js's 211 top-level globals — including all 47 distinct names index.html's inline onclick="fnName(...)" handlers depend on — into closure-private variables, breaking every one of those buttons with no visible error until a click. 17 real "collisions" turned out to be the already-documented, deliberate window.X override pattern (Aug 14's own main.js/hooks bridge fix) — confirmed safe since the override runs inside a deferred onReady() callback, never at parse time. All 32 RPGACE.* references inside main.js confirmed to live inside function bodies, never top-level immediate code — safe regardless of load order. No top-level 'use strict' in main.js — no strict-mode leakage risk either.

Built: main.js's content pasted verbatim OUTSIDE the IIFE, inside a new /* ===LEGACY:mainjs=== *///* ===END:LEGACY:mainjs=== */ marked section near the top of rpgace_core.js — byte-diff-verified identical to the old file. main.js/main.min.js deleted (git history preserves them); index.html now loads exactly one script tag; scripts/minify_client.js re-targeted to the single file.

Real, load-bearing follow-up catch: the merge shifted every original line by exactly 4451, silently breaking scripts/graphify_river_group.py's hardcoded Path('main.js') read — the exact mechanism behind the Aug 14 Galaxy Map UI/backend detection fix (compute_mainjs_window_bridge()). It fails OPEN (empty dict, no crash) rather than loud, so this would have silently regressed authGate/pathRouter back to reading as false-isolated with zero visible error. Fixed with a new _legacy_mainjs_text() helper sourcing the same content from the new sentinel markers — functionally re-verified (authGate's own real bridge still detects correctly, the exact Aug 14 worked example). A real perf regression (re-reading+regex-scanning the now-5x-bigger merged file on every call, unmemoized) timed out galaxy_map_module.py/galaxy_map_zoom.py — fixed with a module-level cache. perspective_generate_onclick_features.py's own separate hardcoded read fixed the same way (shared helper, rule 8). The standing anchor-verification system caught 6 hand-cited line-number anchors gone stale across galaxy_map_decisions.py/galaxy_map_level5.py — exactly its own "fail loud, never silently show stale code" design working as intended — all individually re-verified against live current line numbers (some pure +4451 shift, several genuine independent drift from other same-session edits).

Full 21-script Galaxy Map pipeline regenerated and reverified: idempotent (2 fresh runs, identical md5 across all 22 output files), div/a/svg-balanced, headless-Chromium zero console errors on every touched page (decisions/level5/module/zoom/l0). node --check clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped, oracleAppGrounding.SELF_KNOWLEDGE updated with a real current-architecture note (older dated bullets describing "main.js" as a separate file are left as historical narrative, per this project's own doc-discipline precedent). 1. The load-time complaint had a real, deeper root cause. Real fix: scripts/minify_client.js (terser, RPGACE's first dev-time-only npm devDependency, never shipped to the client or invoked at runtime), compress:true, mangle:false permanently — mangle is disabled because index.html has 93 inline onclick="fnName(...)" attributes referencing 47 distinct globals a per-file minifier can't see, and renaming any of them would silently break that button. Real, measured result: rpgace_core.js 1.1MB → 660KB raw (309KB → 168KB gzipped), main.js 257KB → 208KB raw (75KB → 60KB gzipped). 🔢 Aug 11 (same day, 5th taxonomy pass) — real navigation bug fixed, and the phylum numbers renumbered to match the app's own display order.
Alex hand-tested Phylum Path and pasted the same page twice as proof: clicking into any Order row silently re-rendered the exact same root-level view instead of drilling down. Root cause: phylumPath._loadNodesAndRender had no protection against a real race — the module's own page:show hook listener could re-fire and re-render mid-flight while a direct click handler's own render was still loading, and whichever async response landed LAST silently won regardless of which one was actually requested last. Fixed with a _renderGeneration counter: every call to _loadNodesAndRender stamps a new generation, and any in-flight Supabase response that returns after a newer generation has already started is discarded rather than rendered. `node --check` clean.

Same message, second real ask: "renumber the phylums so they match the chronological order above" — the thematic display grouping (Craft & Production / Knowledge & Mind / Visual & Creative Identity / Business & Distribution / Technology & Misc) didn't match the actual phylum_number values. ♻️ Aug 6 (same day, 3rd pass) — Real /deduplication fix: encyclopedia entries were being re-analyzed on every page load.
Alex spotted the same encyclopedia entries (Harp Arpeggiation, Wiley's Eskimo, Dave x Central Cee, Endshpiel) appearing multiple times in the new "📥 Scouted, Now Answered" list and asked whether entries could be deduplicated against RPGACE's own established content before reprocessing, to save tokens and workload.

Real root cause (direct code read, not guessed): ENC_BULLET_CACHE — the encyclopedia preview-bullet generator's only "cache" — is a plain in-memory JS object with zero persistence. It evaporates on every page reload, so generateEncBullets() re-sent the identical prompt to Oracle every time the Encyclopedia page loaded, even for entries whose bullets already existed from a prior session. Once Fallback Scout mode shipped this same day, those re-generations became re-queued scout_item rows too — which is what Alex actually saw.

Real fix, per rule 8 (dedup always, no exceptions): new encyclopedia.preview_bullets jsonb column (migration applied) — the entry's own real "destination of planned output." generateEncBullets() now checks this persisted column BEFORE ever calling Oracle; a successful generation writes back via RPGACE.sb.secureWrite (encyclopedia is already in api/data-write.js's ALLOWED_TABLES), a failed/fallback generation never does, so a transient error can't poison the row with a useless placeholder bullet.

Second, generic safeguard at the queue layer itself: mockOracle._queueScoutItem now checks existing pending scout_item rows for an exact prompt-text match before inserting, and returns an honest "♻️ already scouted, nothing new queued" acknowledgment instead of minting a duplicate row — caller-agnostic, so it protects every current and future Fallback Scout caller, not just this one root cause.

🐛 Aug 6 — /Engineer real bugfix pass: dangling Oracle-capture race condition, ungated Beat Log auto-fires
Triggered directly by Alex running the full Aug 5 build through a real beat ("Asylum") and pasting the actual transcript: the Beat Log flow auto-fired two giant Oracle prompts back to back with zero human gating, a director pick Oracle genuinely made never became a style_profiles row, and Generate Captions silently failed. Full record: engineer_pass_2026-08-06_10.txt.

Real root cause, traced through actual code, not guessed: visualOracle._captureNextResponse is a bare one-shot listener on the shared oracle:response-scanned hook with no request correlation — and RPGACE.hooks.fire broadcasts to every registered listener (confirmed by direct read). Every real call site armed this capture BEFORE confirming its own sendToOracle() call actually went through — if the in-flight guard silently blocked that send (e.g. a manual click stealing the slot during F18's auto-chain's own async gap), the capture stayed armed and dangling, ready to wrongly consume whatever DIFFERENT reply landed next.

Fixed at the structural level, not papered over: sendToOracle() and the wrapped sendChat() now report whether a send actually went through; all 3 real "arm then send" call sites (Visual Oracle's command panel, F18's auto Visual Treatment chain, and _sendFilledPromptToOracle — the one shared tail Visual Treatment AND Captions both call, so fixing it once fixed both) now send first and only arm if confirmed sent, toasting a specific reason otherwise. Beat Log's two auto-fires became two real, independent, human-gated buttons ("📤 Send to Oracle" / "🎬 Generate Visual Treatment Doc") per Alex's own words — which also removes the unsupervised async window the bug lived in, not just its symptom. Production Panel's Phase 2 buttons relabeled ("New Visual Treatment" / "Reopen Last Visual Treatment") to stop reading as duplicates.

Honest uncertainty, stated plainly: the exact causal chain for the missing style_profiles row is a well-evidenced reconstruction from static code + Supabase state, not a console-verified certainty — Oracle's own compliance with the requested trailer format is a real, separate possible contributor this pass couldn't rule out. The fix closes a real, confirmed structural gap either way. Untested — needs Alex to run the same real flow again.

🐛 July 31 — Round 2: a much bigger, likely long-standing token-cost bug found in the SAME regex, root-caused with real Node evidence before touching anything
Alex re-tested after the first two fixes: the dashDeck answer no longer mis-tagged, but the authGate answer still showed 📸 INSTA-ORACLE with zero Instagram content. Per rule 4 ("one failed fix = stop, get real evidence, don't patch blind a second time"), the actual reply text was tested against the live regex in Node rather than guessed at again.

Confirmed: `story\s` matched literally inside "git history is permanent" — "history" ends in "...story" followed by a space, and the clause has no leading word-boundary check. Checking the sibling clauses the same way surfaced something bigger: `ig\s` matches "big improvement" — the ordinary word "big" followed by a space — and bare `reel` matches inside "freelance"/"freelancer". All three verified directly in Node against real test strings before shipping anything (both "should NOT match" and "should still match" cases checked in both directions).

Real significance: this is very likely a bigger, OLDER contributor to today's earlier "credits dropping fast" complaint than the one-way-latch bug already fixed — "big"/"gig"/"dig" and "history"/"reel"-containing words are common enough in ordinary conversation that this regex has likely been mis-triggering the ~2000-char INSTA_ORACLE_SYS injection on a meaningful fraction of totally unrelated messages for a long time, independent of and probably predating the latch bug.

Fixed by adding a real leading word-boundary (\b) to all three clauses — each still correctly matches its intended standalone usage ("ig story", "post a reel", real Instagram content) while no longer matching as a bare substring inside an unrelated word. Also fixed a real process gap: the earlier same-session #[a-z] fix never bumped main.js's cache-bust query string, so it was genuinely uncertain whether Alex's browser was even running the fixed code — bumped ?v=20260731a → ?v=20260731b this round. FROZEN-file exception, logged. node --check clean.
🧪 July 31 — First real hand-test of the anatomy grounding: 2 real bugs found, both fixed same session
Alex's first real test asked Oracle two questions back to back. "How does dashDeck's popup system work" answered perfectly — cited the real source_ref (rpgace_core.js:5215), the real function names, and the real stash-guard/z-index landmines from the curated row, exactly as designed. "What's the gotcha with authGate" failed — Oracle said it had no grounding and asked Alex to paste the source code, even though authGate IS a curated row in oracle_module_anatomy.

Root cause: ANATOMY_KEYWORDS's original 8 trigger phrases were all "how does X work"-shaped ("how does", "under the hood", "which module handles", etc.) — "what's the gotcha with X" matched none of them, so _buildAnatomyBlock() never even ran; its own real module-name/keyword scoring never got a chance. Fixed by adding broader natural phrasings ("the gotcha", "any gotchas", "tell me about the", "explain the") plus the 13 curated module names themselves (lowercase + spaced-camelCase variants) as direct triggers — naming one of these modules at all is now itself enough to attempt the lookup, regardless of how the question is phrased around it.

Second, separate real bug, surfaced by the first reply, not the second: the correct dashDeck answer got tagged 📸 INSTA-ORACLE despite containing zero Instagram content. This is NOT the one-way-latch bug fixed earlier today — that fix was already correctly working (the flag re-evaluates per-message). The real cause: main.js's isInstaOracleQuery() also checks the reply text itself (isInstaOracleQuery(cleanReply)), and its regex included a bare #[a-z] clause meant to catch Instagram hashtags — which also matches CSS/DOM id references. Oracle's own reply quoted the real element ids from the new anatomy content (#bookworm-widget, #cpl-widget), and those alone tripped the "hashtag" detector. Fixed by removing the bare #[a-z] clause — every other clause (literal "hashtag", "instagram", "@acesanya", etc.) already reliably detects real Instagram-shaped text without this over-broad signal. FROZEN-file exception, logged. node --check clean on both files.

Real lesson: the anatomy content itself (written this session) is what exposed a pre-existing regex weakness in main.js that had apparently never collided with real reply content before — a genuine case of new, correct content surfacing an old, unrelated bug rather than causing one.

Not built this pass — routed to Alex as a real decision (table yes/no, RLS posture, first-batch scope, standing refresh cost), per Council of 5's own rule that a killed-or-reshaped premise gets said plainly rather than quietly built as originally framed.
📸 July 31 — Real token-cost bug fixed: INSTA-ORACLE mode was a one-way latch, never reset
Alex reported credits dropping fast and every Oracle message — including an unrelated mob-movie Visual Treatment Doc — getting tagged 📸 INSTA-ORACLE. Root cause, confirmed by reading main.js directly rather than guessed: window._instaOracleActive was set to true in 5 places but never reset anywhere in the file. Once any single message tripped Instagram-strategist mode, the full ~2,000-character INSTA_ORACLE_SYS block silently kept appending to every later message's system prompt for the rest of the page session, regardless of topic — a real, ongoing token-cost bug, not a one-off.

Fixed by re-evaluating fresh from each message's own content (isInstaOracleQuery(msg)) instead of a persistent flag, so it turns off naturally the moment the topic changes; the explicit one-shot triggers (sendInsightToCmd3 etc.) are unaffected. FROZEN-file exception, logged. node --check clean, cache-bust bumped. Commit 8c56d79, confirmed READY on production.

Same session, a real finding about Oracle's own self-diagnosis, not RPGACE's code: asked directly what was driving the credit drop, Oracle's own chat reply presented several claims with confident, specific-sounding detail (severity ratings, function names) that didn't hold up against the actual source — conversation history was claimed unbounded when main.js already slices to .slice(-8); several DOM/module items conflated browser-rendering or Supabase-read cost with Anthropic token cost, a category error. One claim did hold up and is recorded separately below (prompt caching). Worth remembering generally: Oracle's own "audits" of the app are a hypothesis to verify against real code, same standing rule as everywhere else in this file — not evidence on their own, however confident the formatting looks.

🎬 July 31 — First real openmontage_jobs round-trip: the separate session set up cleanly and correctly failed loud
The OpenMontage Claude Code session picked up the Calibri job. Real setup all worked: Python venv, pip/npm installs, FFmpeg. It correctly chose the cinematic pipeline over documentary-montage from the brief alone.

Then it refused to render, and the refusal was the right call. The brief needs a specific recurring character across scenes (a mob shootout, a car chase) — and generic stock/archival footage cannot depict a person who has to stay the same person from shot to shot. It marked the job status='failed' with an honest, detailed output_note rather than shipping something wrong. This is the first real proof that the "fail loud, never fake success" discipline holds across a session boundary, in a repo this session cannot even see.

Real advisory finding (Council of 5 + Aintergration): none of OpenMontage's supported providers — FAL, OpenAI, Google, Runway, Kling, xAI — natively guarantee character consistency across separately-generated scenes. That is a genuine limitation of the current generation landscape, not a configuration mistake, and it holds regardless of which provider gets picked. HeyGen was considered and is a mismatch for this brief specifically (an avatar/talking-head tool, not a narrative cinematic one).

Shipped as a result, deliberately provider-independent: visualOracle.CMDS[1]'s Visual Treatment Doc prompt now always asks for a Character Reference Block — a fixed physical description of any recurring character, meant to be handed to image generation as a consistent per-scene reference. A failure on the far shore flowed back and reshaped the prompt it came from, which is the entire argument for treating this as a real two-way channel rather than a place jobs disappear into. Findings were written back into the shared openmontage_jobs row so both sessions read one timeline. node --check clean.

Open, and genuinely Alex's call: which specific paid provider (if any) to configure. That is a Tier-3 spend decision and was put to him directly rather than defaulted. Until one is configured, RPGACE briefs and hands off — it does not generate video.

🎤 July 29 (Round 45) — Real second attempt at voice-input duplication: Round 43's fix (e.resultIndex) confirmed NOT sufficient by a real reload-and-retest
Alex reloaded and retested per Round 44's own ask — the exact same compounding duplication ("can you" → "can you can you" → ...) still happened on the new floating button. This rules out the "stale tab" theory and means the e.resultIndex-based fix genuinely didn't work in practice.

Real reasoning for why, not a second blind guess: Android Chrome's SpeechRecognition (what Alex uses) is documented to be far less spec-reliable than desktop Chrome specifically around e.resultIndex and continuous-mode restarts — relying on the browser's own "what changed" hint was the wrong foundation to build on.

Rewrite: ignores e.resultIndex entirely. Tracks idempotency ourselves via array position (_finalizedCount) — once e.results[i].isFinal is true, that transcript is committed to _baseText exactly once, regardless of what the browser claims changed. Also spawns a genuinely NEW SpeechRecognition object on every auto-restart instead of reusing the ended one — the standard, more robust pattern real SpeechRecognition wrapper libraries use, since reusing an ended instance is inconsistent on some mobile implementations.

`node --check` clean, cache-bust bumped 20260729a→20260729b. Per CLAUDE.md rule 9 / the Engineer skill's per-defect cap: this is the last attempt before stopping to get real console evidence instead of guessing a third time — if this doesn't hold up on a real retest, next step needs Alex's actual browser console output, not another reasoned-but-unverified patch.

🎤 July 28/29 (Round 43) — Real bug caught from Alex's own first hand-test of voice input: transcript duplication, root-caused and fixed same night
Alex tested the new 🎤 button (Round 41) and pasted the real result: a BeatStars listing generated correctly by Insta-Oracle, but with a gibberish "hello hello hello can hello can you hear me, I said once hello can you hear me" block appearing under an otherwise-empty section — his own mic test getting mangled and submitted as a follow-up Oracle message, not anything Oracle itself generated.

Real root cause: voiceInput._start's rec.onresult looped e.results from index 0 on every single event. Chrome's continuous mode never shrinks e.results — it keeps every result (final and interim) for the life of the recognition session — so each event re-summed ALL final text seen so far and then appended that full running sum onto an already-appended _baseText. A compounding duplication: one final word became "word", then the next event re-added it as "word word", then a third as "word word word <new word>" — exactly the pattern in Alex's paste.

Fix: loop from e.resultIndex instead of 0 — the browser-provided index of the first result that actually changed since the last event, the standard Web Speech API idiom. One-line, well-understood, low-risk (Tier 1, single function) — fixed the same night rather than left live, per this project's standing practice of acting immediately on real hand-test bugs.

`node --check` clean, cache-bust bumped 20260728j→20260728k. Untested — needs Alex's real second hand-test (same 🎤 button) to confirm the fix holds; the first test is what caught this, so it already proved the button itself opens/records — just the accumulation logic was wrong.
🎧 July 28 (Round 42) — /Routine item 5: local librosa audio analysis built, real evidence changed the design from the naive assumption
Alex's answer to the Cyanite.ai-vs-librosa-vs-neither fork (logged Round 40): "Local librosa (free)."

Real evidence check first (rule 1/8) caught a wrong assumption before any code was written. Grepped for the existing local-server integration pattern (main.js:4057's LOCAL_SERVER constant) expecting a direct browser→localhost fetch — found it's dead code, exactly one match, the definition itself. The real, live pattern Content Intelligence actually uses is a Supabase async job queue (intel_jobs, confirmed anon_all RLS via a direct pg_policies query) that local_server.py polls on its own clock. Building a direct-fetch design instead would have been a second, divergent, wrong pattern for talking to the same local server (rule 8) — caught before it happened, not after.

Built, following the real intel_jobs shape: new beat_audio_jobs table + a new beat-audio Storage bucket (Supabase Storage was completely unused in this project before today — confirmed empty), both anon_all, same reasoning as intel_jobs (local_server.py only ever holds the anon key). Tier-3 migration — confirmed with Alex before applying, not assumed from his earlier general "local librosa" answer. rpgace_core.js's beatLog module gained _tryRealAudioAnalysis: uploads the real dropped/picked File's bytes, inserts a job row with a client-generated uuid (sidesteps the "insert doesn't return the row" landmine entirely), polls up to 90s, and on completion fills BPM + Key/Scale — honestly scoped to Major/Minor only, since that's what a chromagram + Krumhansl-Schmuckler correlation can actually discriminate; exotic modes (Dorian/Phrygian/etc) stay manual. Fails completely silently if local_server.py never picks the job up (not running, or Alex is on his phone with the PC off) — the instant filename-guess from _parseFilename is never blocked or overwritten.

Full record: beat_log_matching_spec_backlog_2026-07-28.txt.

🧭 July 28 (Round 34) — Real root cause found for Oracle's overclaim: the self-audit command never received its own grounding data. Plus a live regression across 3 Oracle wraps, fixed.

A real, live regression found and fixed in the same pass: tracing this required reading all three modules that wrap window.callOracle (oracleTreeGrounding, oracleAppGrounding, oracleFetchGuard) — every one of them, when its own gate matched, forwarded a fixed 3-argument call (orig.call(this, messages, modifiedSystem, maxTokens)), silently dropping any 4th argument. Today's streaming fix added exactly a 4th argument (onChunk) to callOracle() — meaning any time one of these three grounding/guard mechanisms matched on a real message, streaming would silently, invisibly fall back to blocking behavior for that turn. Fixed all three (rule 8 — one shared bug, not three separate patches) to rebuild the full arguments array instead of a fixed-arity forward, verified with a standalone simulation confirming the callback survives being passed through all three chained wraps.

📡 July 28 (Round 33) — Real hand-test feedback acted on same-session: streaming was unreadable, and Oracle's own flagged 5thDimension reply exposed 2 real gaps
Alex used the new streaming Oracle for real and reported the text appearing "bit by bit, but not very readable" — a real, immediate hand-test finding on the just-shipped 504 fix. Root cause: sendChat()'s streaming preview used .textContent, which collapses every newline — a structured reply (headers, lists, paragraph breaks) streamed in as one unbroken wall of text until the FINAL render swap (renderMarkdown() → innerHTML) made it readable all at once. Fixed by running the same, already-XSS-safe renderMarkdown() on every chunk instead of just the final one — the preview now looks properly formatted the whole time it streams, not only at completion. Worst case (an incomplete **bold marker mid-word for one chunk) self-corrects the instant the closing marker streams in — a normal, acceptable streaming-UI artifact.

Separately, Alex used the new "🧪 Flag for Claude Code" button on Insta-Oracle's own 5thDimension self-audit — the first real use of the whole Oracle-dev-bridge loop this session. Reviewing it surfaced two real things: (1) a genuine oracleDevBridge._flag() bug — the flagged text was silently truncated at exactly 4000 characters mid-sentence, losing Oracle's own conclusion, with zero indication to Alex anything was cut. Fixed: raised to 8000 (comfortably covers a max_tokens:1200 reply) with an explicit truncation marker if a reply still somehow exceeds it. (2) A real misalignment between what Oracle's self-audit claimed and actual code state — see the same-session chat reply for the full breakdown (Oracle called the 21-Phylum tree "structurally complete" when only 10 are actually built via the Development Framework; and Oracle's own "quest tracking might be unreliable" counter-case understated the real gap — the Quest Board's addXP()/completeQuest() has zero persistence at all today, a separate, still-unfixed instance of the same in-memory-only problem the July 22 career-score rebuild already fixed for the dashboard's own XP display).

⚡ July 28 (Round 31) — Real Oracle 504 fix, second attempt: genuine server-backed streaming, replacing a client-only stub that never worked
Item 6 of today's `/Routine` Top 10, now unblocked (credits reset). Alex asked to extend the Vercel function window or find a workaround. Real evidence-gathering first (per rule 4 — "one failed fix = stop, get real evidence" — this is literally the second attempt at this exact bug): read the dead `RPGACE.streamOracle`/`restoreSendChat` code CLAUDE.md already flagged as neutralised-not-removed, and found the actual reason the first attempt broke — api/oracle.js never looked at a stream field at all, so the client's SSE parser (waiting for data: lines) was consuming a single non-streaming JSON blob that never matched, producing a permanently empty buffer on every message. The client-side parsing logic itself was already correct against Anthropic's real wire format; it just never received real stream data.

The real fix, this time genuinely end-to-end: api/oracle.js now proxies Anthropic's actual SSE stream byte-for-byte when the caller sends stream:true (images excluded — a deliberate, named scope limit, not an oversight). main.js's callOracle() gained an optional 4th onChunk parameter — every one of its ~20 other call sites is completely unaffected, since omitting it keeps the exact prior blocking behavior and return shape. sendChat()'s one direct-Oracle branch now passes a callback that progressively fills the existing typing-indicator bubble as text arrives — every other side effect (quest-suggestion parsing, XP, Save-to-Journal/Encyclopedia buttons, chat history) still runs unchanged on the final resolved text, since only the temporary preview element is touched mid-stream. This is deliberately narrower than the first attempt, which fully reimplemented sendChat in parallel and silently dropped all of that functionality — the exact failure class being avoided this time.

Dead code actually removed, not re-flagged a second time: RPGACE.streamOracle, its sendChat intercept, and the whole restoreSendChat module are deleted from rpgace_core.js (CLAUDE.md's own standing tech-debt item, closed rather than carried forward again) — keeping the old, differently-broken mechanism around risked a future session reviving the wrong one by mistake. Module count corrected 52→51 in system_flow_map.md as a direct consequence.

maxDuration raised 60→300 at Alex's explicit request — confirmed real, checked directly rather than assumed: list_deployments shows commit c296471 READY on production with this value in place, meaning Vercel accepted the deploy (this account's plan allows 300s, it isn't Hobby-capped at 60). Streaming is still the more load-bearing half of the fix regardless: even a response that hits the 300s ceiling now shows its content as it's generated, instead of a blank wait followed by nothing.

Honest limitation, stated plainly: this session has no login credentials for the live app and cannot hand-test the real chat UI. Everything above is `node --check`-clean and traced line-by-line against the real diff, not assumed — but real end-to-end verification (does the typing bubble actually fill in smoothly, does a genuinely long response now complete instead of 504'ing) needs Alex's own hand-test, the same real gap already named for this week's animation work.
🔒 July 28 (Round 30) — Real XSS finding fixed: Oracle chat's renderMarkdown() injected unescaped HTML, closing the audit item flagged 3 separate times since July 22
Executing item 7 of the day's `/Routine` Top 10, the same "whether fetched external content ever reaches `innerHTML` unescaped" question CLAUDE.md had flagged and deferred since July 22 without ever being investigated. Real audit, not another guess: grepped every innerHTML = assignment across both files (217 total sites) for ones handling text likely to carry externally-sourced content, and found the actual bug — main.js's renderMarkdown(text) is the render function behind EVERY Oracle chat message (used by addMsg() for every AI/system reply, renderInstaMsg() for Insta-Oracle, and rpgace_core.js's streaming-message renderer via window.renderMarkdown) — and it ran its markdown regex replacements directly on the raw response text with zero HTML-escaping first. Since Oracle responses can carry fetched web/YouTube/Instagram content (per oracleFetchGuard's own documented risk — that guard tells the model to treat fetched content as data, but can't stop the model quoting hostile HTML-like text back verbatim), any literal <script>/<img onerror=...>-shaped text arriving in a response would have rendered/executed directly in the chat DOM.

Fix, logged FROZEN-file exception (same pattern as `checkPassword()`'s prior exceptions): a new local _escChatHtml() escapes &/</> before renderMarkdown's existing regex chain runs — verified the markdown syntax (**bold**, *italic*, `` `code` ``, headings, lists, `---`) is unaffected since none of those regexes touch the three escaped characters, and a test string containing a literal <script> tag now renders as inert escaped text instead of live markup. node --check main.js clean.

Real scope note: a second, lower-severity site (a local daily-log renderer, main.js ~line 1952) reads from localStorage only — the user's own device data, not externally-fetched content — left untouched as out of scope for this pass, same discipline as not over-fixing beyond the actual flagged risk.

✅ July 25 (Round 27) — The 433-vs-198 color drift fixed for real, not left as the "flagged, not yet fixed" future project
Alex: "do it, /scope and /Godmode" — closing the one item Round 24's Aintergration audit had explicitly deferred. Real evidence first: DESIGN.md's own documented semantic colors exactly match style.css's real tokens, confirming the 433 hardcoded hexes in rpgace_core.js were the drift, not a second deliberate system.

A real alternative theory was checked and ruled out before touching anything: these same bright values also appear in patch_notes.html's own separate :root block, which could have meant two intentionally-separate color systems. Checking actual usage showed most hardcoded occurrences are arguments to RPGACE.utils.toast(msg, color, ms), which does color + '40' string concatenation for the alpha channel — a straight var(--token) substitution would have produced invalid CSS (var(--red)40) at these exact call sites. Fixed with a literal hex-value correction instead, syntactically identical to the drift, safe regardless of the concatenation.

5 color families reconciled, 326 of 433 occurrences: #E25454→#CC4A4A (94), #E2E2EC→#D4DAF5 (90), #3DAA6E→#4CAF82 (69), #4A90E2→#4A8CCC (43), #9B59B6→#9B6EC8 (30). Gold (#C9A84C, 35 occurrences) left untouched — already an exact match. Total hex count confirmed unchanged (433→433) before/after, proving a pure value swap with no corruption. node --check clean; cache-bust bumped (20260725a→20260725b) per /Engineer's own G7 gate.

Committed c2bc324/2d4dac2, merged to both branches, both confirmed READY on production via list_deployments. ~72 remaining hex occurrences (21 distinct values with no matching token) deliberately left out of scope — promoting those to new named tokens is a real, separate future decision, not something to fold in silently.

✅ July 24 (Round 22) — No more post-login pop-in (all boot-time injectors gated) + taxonomy review-queue badge fixed for real
Alex, verbatim: "no loads should happen after login, only before." Fix: a new shared gate, RPGACE._bootTasks/RPGACE.registerBootTask(fn), generalizing the one existing precedent (careerStatCard._fetchAll(), July 23) to every real boot-time injector in the file. The boot loader's hide logic now does Promise.all(R._bootTasks) before hiding instead of waiting on just one module. The earlier fix never actually worked live. Per the standing rule (one failed fix = stop, get real evidence, don't patch blind a second time), this was not re-patched — the floating-badge module was deleted and folded into dashDeck's already-working "Needs you now" list, which also closed a real second gap: that list only ever counted taxonomy_proposals, never taxonomy_links (pending fusion links) — two hand-rolled copies of one feature, one broken, one incomplete (rule 8). taxonomyReviewQueue keeps _openQueue() and its accept/reject popup logic, which dashDeck's list item already calls into.

🧊 July 24 (Round 19) — FROZEN-file exception: main.js migrated to the authenticated write-proxy (logged, Alex-confirmed)
Alex confirmed both steps of the RLS-flip plan, in order: migrate main.js first, then flip RLS. This card covers step 1 — the deliberate, logged FROZEN-file exception CLAUDE.md's own architecture rule requires. node --check main.js clean before commit, per that same rule.

A dedicated Opus 5 audit (real GODMODE evidence pass, read-only) enumerated all 12 real write call sites among main.js's 27 raw Supabase calls and found the migration wasn't a pure mechanical swap: saveOracleToEncyclopedia's upsert (?on_conflict=title, merge-duplicates) had no equivalent in api/data-write.js — a blind swap would have turned every re-save of an existing title into a 409 that the proxy throws on, silently breaking the VST-tag write that only ever runs after a successful save (the exact bug this upsert already exists to prevent). Fixed by adding an optional onConflict param to both api/data-write.js and RPGACE.sb.secureWrite's signature, used only by that one call site.

All 8 physical edit points migrated: scheduleToCalendar (→ rpgace_agendas, also fixed a real RPGACE.DB bypass on the same function's localStorage write), saveOracleToEncyclopedia (→ encyclopedia upsert), sbInsightPost/sbInsightDelete (→ encyclopedia_insights, single choke points covering 3 UI triggers), learnVideo and submitIntelURL (→ intel_jobs), saveEncWithVSTs (→ encyclopedia patch), deleteEncEntry/clearEncyclopedia, saveToJournal/deleteJournalEntry/clearJournal (→ journal, the widest trigger set — 9 real call sites across both files). journal, encyclopedia_insights, rpgace_agendas, intel_jobs added to api/data-write.js's ALLOWED_TABLES — this closes the doc-correction from Round 18: these 4 tables' "zero client write call sites" claim was wrong specifically because main.js was never grepped.

Real, deliberate scope limit — not an oversight: the same audit found journal has a genuine external writer (the Morning Brief Routine, plain anon key, no service-role access) and intel_jobs/intel_reports/intel_watchlist have a genuine external writer too (a local Python server on Alex's own machine, port 7842, also anon-key-only). Both of RPGACE's own client call sites for these 4 tables now route through the authenticated proxy for consistency, but **their RLS is deliberately staying anon_all** — same treatment as the already-documented oracle_fallback_queue landmine. Flipping any of these 4 would silently break a real system this session cannot fix (an external Routine or a script outside this repo). Two unfiltered delete-all patterns (clearEncyclopedia, clearJournal) were migrated mechanically, same real blast radius as today (still gated only by a browser confirm(), no Supabase backup/PITR exists) — flagged, not silently glossed over.

Next: the RLS flip itself, on the remaining tables that have no external writer.
🔓 July 24 (Round 18) — /Routine interconnection debate: the RLS flip was never actually done, and 9 real interconnection/dedup fixes shipped
Alex asked for a dedicated /Routine pass scoped to "debugs, interconnections, and deduplications" — two independent Opus 5 agents (Team 1: evidence-based case for RPGACE's most vital/load-bearing components; Team 2: honest counter-case) ran in parallel, each querying live Supabase and grepping the real codebase directly. Both independently found the same thing, unprompted: every one of the 19 tables in Approach B's ALLOWED_TABLES still runs anon_all — the RLS flip this project has described as "live" or "a real restriction" across multiple prior rounds was never actually executed. The write-proxy code is real and correctly gated; the anon key (public in this repo) still has full unrestricted direct access to every one of those tables. See CLAUDE.md's corrected Security Note for the full, blunt correction.

Real ordering constraint found in the same pass: main.js makes 27 raw Supabase calls with the anon key that never route through secureWrite — including 8 real writes across the exact 4 tables (journal/rpgace_agendas/intel_jobs/encyclopedia_insights) this doc previously and incorrectly claimed had "zero real client write call sites." Flipping RLS before migrating those would break real live writes. The RLS flip itself is Tier 3 and still needs Alex's explicit go-ahead — not done this pass, correctly held pending confirmation.

Nine real fixes shipped this pass (auto-implemented, no destructive risk):
  1. RPGACE.sb.secureWrite never invalidated cache — 66 call sites across 15 tables were silently serving stale reads for up to 60s after every write. Now wrapped like insert/update/del already were.
  2. Fixed a real typo bug: the cache's own noCache exemption list said 'journal_entries' — a table that doesn't exist. Real table is journal; it was never actually exempted.
  3. taxonomyTree._generateNodeContent hand-rolled its own /api/oracle fetch instead of routing through the shared ground-worker choke point — meaning it silently wrote empty content as "success" on any API failure. Likely the real cause of the long-open "empty deep_content" mystery. Now routes through phylumPath._callGroundWorkerText.
  4. RPGACE.hooks.fire()/pipe() now iterate a snapshot of the listener array, not the live one — closes a sharper, previously-undocumented hazard where on()'s in-place .sort() could skip not-yet-visited listeners mid-fire (currently masked since every real site uses the same default priority, but a real future landmine).
  5. All 24 confirmed-dead hooks.on('rpgace:ready', ...) re-subscriptions fixed — 14 were genuinely harmless (an identical direct call already ran elsewhere in the same init()) and were deleted; ~10 had NO other boot path at all and were unwrapped into real direct calls, including intelDelete's entire dead MutationObserver setup (new research-page cards never got their buttons injected without a page navigation) and youtubeOracle's dead button injector (which also had a page-name bug — checked the literal 'oracle', which never matches the real page id 'advisor').
  6. Fixed a real config-layer conflict behind the still-open Oracle 504 bug: api/oracle.js's own maxDuration said 60, vercel.json's said 30 for the same file — verified via Vercel's own docs that a file's own export const config is the standard/canonical approach; removed the conflicting vercel.json entry.
  7. Deleted 3 confirmed-dead duplicate files: oracle_streaming.js (zero references anywhere), rpgace_config.js (an orphaned stale copy of the real config module), api_lastfm.js (a pre-security-fix duplicate of api/lastfm.js missing requireAuth — confirmed by diff, not routed by Vercel so never live-exposed, but a real drift artifact).
  8. Unified two divergent HTML-escape implementations — one escaped only <, unsafe in attribute context — into a single shared RPGACE.utils.escapeHtml.
  9. Removed intelDelete's own unused hardcoded Supabase credential copy (confirmed zero reads via grep) and fixed 2 real writes (shift sync, schedule-agenda sync) that bypassed RPGACE.DB and silently skipped the db:change hook — any future listener on that hook would never have learned those two tables changed.
node --check clean throughout; vercel.json confirmed valid JSON. Remaining items (the RLS flip + main.js migration, the XSS audit) need Alex's input — see the question below this card in chat, not silently assumed.

🔒 July 23 (Round 6) — API auth + password moved server-side (Tier 3, Alex-authorized "do now"); a real deduplication bug found and fixed along the way; NOT yet deployed to main
Alex's real answer to /5thDimension's three Phase 5 forks: "1. do now 2. reduplication is always a priority... same processes must go through one pipeline or function if steps are identical 3. yes make live, always automate where it makes improvements." (term corrected same day — he meant "deduplication," the word this project's own code already uses; kept verbatim above since it's a direct quote.)

#1 built: the API-auth + client-side-password gap named back at the July 20/22 Aintergration audit is fixed as one real architecture change. New api/auth.js checks the password server-side against process.env.CORRECT_PW (never shipped to the client) and returns a shared secret (process.env.RPGACE_API_SECRET). A new requireAuth() in api/_context.js is called by every other endpoint. A new authGate module (rpgace_core.js) installs a single global fetch() wrap that attaches the secret to every /api/* call — one real choke point instead of touching all ~26 existing call sites across main.js and rpgace_core.js individually, directly applying #2's own principle to this exact build. main.js's checkPassword() is a deliberate, logged FROZEN-file exception: it no longer holds the real password as a string at all, just awaits the new server check. A real latent bug was caught and fixed along the way: the existing login-hook wrap fired unconditionally on every attempt, including wrong passwords — now it only fires on genuine success.

#2 (deduplication) immediately found a real, concrete bug while building #1, not just a style complaint: api/composio.js had its own hand-rolled CORS headers AND its own ACCOUNTS map that had drifted from _context.js's copy — composio.js's gmail/instagram connected-account ids were newer and correct (its own comment said "UPDATED June 28 2026"), while _context.js's were stale. Since executor.js/orchestrate.js both read _context.js's copy via callComposio(), any Gmail/Instagram action routed through those two files had silently been hitting the WRONG connected account since June 28 — no error, no warning. Fixed: _context.js is now the single source of truth for ACCOUNTS/TOOL_ALIASES/CORS, and composio.js/search.js import from it instead of keeping their own copies. Promoted to a new standing CLAUDE.md rule (#8), verbatim quote included, this exact bug as the worked example.

#3 (make Oracle's self-awareness live instead of hand-maintained) is confirmed but deliberately NOT built this round — a real, separate architecture project, not rushed alongside an already-large security fix in the same turn.

Real, honest deployment status: all 12 touched api/*.js files pass node --check. Tested with a minimal Vercel-shaped mock server dynamically importing the real api/auth.js/_context.js (wrong password rejected, correct password returns the secret, a protected stub endpoint 401s without the header and succeeds with it), then a real headless Playwright run through the actual login UI end-to-end, confirming a genuine fetch('/api/oracle') call carried the header via the global wrap. This is sitting on the feature branch, not merged to main — it needs Alex to set two real Vercel environment variables (CORRECT_PW, RPGACE_API_SECRET) first, since deploying without them would break login and every API call in production, and no fallback value was hardcoded anywhere (this repo is public — a hardcoded fallback secret would be exactly as exposed as the problem being fixed). A deliberate, reasoned exception to the usual "merge before telling Alex to test" rule, since here that rule's real intent (never leave Alex locked out of his own app) is better served by waiting.

XP/Level/"Done" DO have real code (STATE.xp/totalXP/tasksCompleted, addXP(), levelUp()) but STATE lives only in memory — no localStorage, no Supabase write anywhere — so it silently resets to zero on every single page load. The screenshot Alex pasted ("0 Done / 1 Streak / 1 Level / 0 Total XP") was just the default state, not a snapshot of stalled progress — the card had never once reflected anything real.

Meanwhile real, persisted evidence of career-path progress already existed, untouched, in Supabase — checked directly (not guessed): 37 journal entries, 106 encyclopedia insights, 9 accepted taxonomy proposals, 3 completed Bookworm chapters, 32 reference tracks, 5 content_productions rows. Real, honest finding also worth logging: 0 of those 5 content_productions rows have ever actually been posted to a real platform (no YouTube/Instagram/TikTok URL on any row, despite one being marked "Analysed") — directly reinforces the existing "Beat Log has never once persisted" / F16-F18-never-hand-tested finding from the July 20 audit.

Council of 5 — real pushback before building: (1) Goodhart's-law risk — a single blended score would let busywork in the taxonomy tree count the same as actually shipping a beat, rewarding the wrong thing. Fixed by keeping Output (real shipped content only) and Growth (learning/knowledge-tree small wins) as two separate bars — Alex can see at a glance whether he's shipping or just building/studying, matching the governing rule's own structure instead of blurring it. (2) Considered and explicitly rejected: variable-ratio/randomised reward schedules (the mobile-game "loot box" mechanic) — genuinely effective at driving compulsive engagement, which is exactly the wrong shape for a tool whose governing rule is real output, not engagement-farming. Not built, on purpose. (3) bookworm_chapters counts toward the cumulative Growth total but is excluded from the streak calculation and the new recent-wins feed — confirmed via direct query that a book's chapter rows all share one bulk-insert timestamp from TOC detection, not real per-chapter completion time; using it for "recent activity" would have shown a misleading date. (4) Scope was deliberately held to the stat card itself — not a new badges/achievements subsystem, not a rewrite of the existing hardcoded daily/weekly quest board (left as-is, flagged as a separate future task, not touched here).

What's real now: a new careerStatCard module (rpgace_core.js) computes, on every load: a weighted lifetime XP total from real row counts (shipped content 100pt, content ideas 5pt, journal entries 8pt, insights 4pt, accepted proposals 6pt, completed chapters 15pt, reference tracks 3pt), a real Level/title using the same cumulative bands as the original per-level requirements, a real day-streak (Duolingo-style — alive if something real happened today OR yesterday), and a "Recent Wins" feed (new, directly under the stat card, always visible) showing the last 5 real actions with relative timestamps — the actual reaffirming-small-wins mechanic Alex asked for. HP/MP relabeled ⚔ OUTPUT / 🧠 GROWTH (same bar UI Alex already likes, now wired to something real instead of two dead bars). No main.js edit — everything reads Supabase directly and overwrites the same DOM ids main.js's own (now-superseded) ephemeral logic used to own; a `xp:awarded` hook listener re-syncs real numbers immediately after any old quest-board click, so the two systems can't visibly fight each other. Every Supabase read fails open (empty array, not a crash) if a query ever fails.

node --check rpgace_core.js clean. Real Supabase counts confirmed via direct query (not estimated): current real lifetime total ≈ 940 XP, which lands at real Level 2 ("Content Apprentice") — genuine reflection of the taxonomy/Bookworm/research work already done this month, even though Output (real shipped content) honestly reads 0/3. Fail-open path verified with a real headless Playwright run against a blocked-network environment: zero page errors, all fields render honest zero-state values instead of hanging or crashing. The happy path (real numbers against live Supabase) has NOT been hand-tested by Alex yet — needs a real browser load to confirm.
📱 July 22 — RPGACE is now a real installable PWA (Android/desktop "Add to Home Screen"), plus a boot-loader screen so the login gate never appears mid-race
Two requests, one session: "turn RPGACE into an app" + "add a loading screen so all DOMs are fully loaded before the login screen appears."

1. PWA. Added manifest.json (name/icons/standalone display/dark theme colour matching the app's own --dark/--gold tokens) and sw.js (service worker) at the repo root, plus a new icons/ folder with a generated gold-sword mark on a dark rounded square (192/512/maskable variants + apple-touch-icon + favicons), matching the gate's own ⚔ branding rather than a generic placeholder. Registration lives in a new pwaInstall module in rpgace_core.js — no new <script> tag (the two-script landmine stays intact), registers immediately at parse time since it has zero dependency on RPGACE.CONFIG or login.

Real gotcha caught before shipping: vercel.json's existing catch-all rewrite ("/(.*)" → "/index.html", needed for the SPA-style page routing) would have silently served index.html for every request to /manifest.json, /sw.js, and /icons/* too — breaking installability entirely with no visible error. Added explicit passthrough rewrites ahead of the catch-all, same pattern the existing /api/* routes already use.

Service worker is deliberately NOT a naive offline-first cache. Today's own earlier incident (a stale rpgace_core.js?v= silently serving old code) is exactly the bug class a cache-first SW would make permanent instead of "until next hard refresh." sw.js is network-first for index.html/rpgace_core.js/main.js/style.css (falls back to cache only when truly offline), cache-first only for the icons/manifest (which never change without a filename bump), and never intercepts /api/* at all.

2. Boot loader. A full-screen overlay (#rpgace-boot-loader, raw HTML+CSS in index.html, visible at first paint — before the login gate, before any script has run) now covers the screen until rpgace_core.js's rpgace:ready fire point confirms every module has registered, at which point it's hidden and the password gate appears. A pure-CSS 6-second fallback animation (zero JS dependency) guarantees it can never trap the user if script execution ever stalls — same "fail loud, never silently hang" principle as the rest of the app. In practice this resolves in well under a second on a normal connection (confirmed via a real headless Playwright run: boot loader hides, gate appears, login succeeds, and the left nav drawer opens with all 8 items on the very first click post-login, no lag) — it does NOT wait for the various 1200-8000ms delayed content-injections individual modules run afterward (Phylum Path's shell, dashDeck cards, etc.) since those are page-specific lazy content, not core readiness, and waiting for all of them would trade an intermittent nav-lag for a guaranteed multi-second wait on every single login, which is a worse trade, not a better one.

node --check rpgace_core.js clean. Real headless Playwright smoke test run and passed (not just code-review). Div-balance re-checked (232/231 same off-by-one gap as the pre-existing July 20 finding, not a new imbalance). Android install: open rpgace.vercel.app in Chrome → menu (⋮) → "Add to Home Screen" / "Install app" (Chrome may also prompt automatically after the manifest+SW are detected). Not yet hand-tested by Alex on a real phone.
🐢 July 22 — Real root cause found for "left nav doesn't respond for ~10s after login": onReady() was waiting for the wrong signal, not just for leftNav specifically
Alex's report: page loads, dashboard scrolls fine, but clicking anything in the left list does nothing for the first ~10 seconds. GODMODE evidence pass instead of another guess.

Real root cause: onReady() — the function every one of ~40 modules' init() calls wait behind via rpgace:ready — gated on document.readyState==='complete' (the window 'load' event), which waits for EVERY subresource on the page (every image, every font) to finish downloading, not just the DOM. The comment justifying this said "give initApp() time to run" — checked checkPassword() in main.js directly: initApp() is called from the login handler, gated entirely by WHEN Alex logs in, completely decoupled from page-load timing. It can run long after window.load already fired, or need to run before it ever will. There was never a real dependency on waiting for images/fonts — just unnecessary latency stacked in front of every module's startup, worst-case as long as the slowest image/font on the page takes.

Fix: onReady() now checks document.readyState !== 'loading' (DOMContentLoaded-equivalent — fires once the DOM itself is parsed, which it already is by the time rpgace_core.js runs, since both scripts load at the very end of <body>) instead of waiting for full 'complete', cutting the delay from window.load + 150ms to effectively DOMContentLoaded + 50ms. This is a systemic fix — every module's startup timing improves, not just leftNav's.

Second, separate bug found in the same pass: leftNav._go(page) unconditionally dereferenced RPGACE.CONFIG.pages.phylumPath on every single click, regardless of which page was clicked. If RPGACE.CONFIG ever wasn't ready yet at click time, this threw before ever calling showPage() — the click would silently do nothing, no error shown, no toast, exactly matching Alex's description. Guarded defensively, same pattern as _items()'s existing CONFIG guard.

Stale cache-busting version found again — the version string was bumped once already today (?v=20260722a) after the earlier incident, but rpgace_core.js changed again after that bump for this fix, so it was bumped a second time to ?v=20260722b. Repeating the lesson from earlier today: bump this every time rpgace_core.js changes, not just once per day.

node --check rpgace_core.js clean. Merged to main and pushed before this card was written, per the standing rule. Not yet hand-tested — needs a real hard-refresh + login + immediate click-through from Alex to confirm the lag is actually gone.
🚨 July 22 — Real bug caught live: the "instant sidebar" fix briefly broke the whole app, root-caused and properly fixed; stale cache-busting version found and bumped
Alex caught this live from the browser console within minutes of the earlier QoL pass shipping — worth logging honestly, not just the fix.

What broke: the "sidebar takes ages to load" fix made leftNav._buildDrawer() run immediately at script-parse time, outside the normal rpgace:ready gate. _buildDrawer() reads RPGACE.CONFIG.pages, and RPGACE.CONFIG isn't set until the config module's own init() runs, much later in the file. The uncaught exception aborted the rest of rpgace_core.js's synchronous execution entirely — confirmed live: only 15 of ~40 modules had registered, including config itself never running, which cascaded into a second visible error (RPGACE.ui.batchList undefined, since uiBatchList's definition sits later in the file and never got to run either).

First fix (hotfix) reverted _buildDrawer() back to running inside the normal gated init(), keeping only the CONFIG-independent _injectStyles()/_injectHamburger() calls instant. This stopped the catastrophic full-file abort — but a second, real, pre-existing bug then surfaced on its own: leftNav registers earlier in this file than config, so leftNav.init() has ALWAYS run before config.init() sets up RPGACE.CONFIG, every single page load, since leftNav shipped July 20. This was never actually fixed before — it silently self-healed via toggle()'s own lazy-rebuild fallback (by the time a user actually clicks the hamburger, config.init() has long since run within the same synchronous rpgace:ready pass). Alex was seeing the console error only because the earlier crash prompted closer console-watching, not because anything new broke.

Real fix: _items() now guards on RPGACE.CONFIG actually existing (returns [] instead of throwing); _buildDrawer() no longer caches an empty shell when that happens, so a new setTimeout(300) retry in init() gets a genuine second chance once the same rpgace:ready pass has finished running every module's queued listener, config included.

Separately found: a real deployment gap. index.html's script tag was still rpgace_core.js?v=20260716a — that cache-busting version string hadn't been bumped once despite the file being edited dozens of times today. Any browser with an already-cached copy of that exact URL would never have picked up any of today's fixes without a hard refresh. Bumped to ?v=20260722a. Worth remembering going forward: bump this version string whenever rpgace_core.js changes, or returning visitors silently run stale code indefinitely.

node --check rpgace_core.js clean. Confirmed via Vercel's real runtime-error logs (not assumed) that the /api/oracle 500 errors seen alongside this were unrelated — an Anthropic usage-limit cap hit since July 19, resetting Aug 1, nothing fixable in code.
🧹 July 22 — QoL pass 2: shift-sync cache fixed for real, Agents folded into Oracle AI, Encyclopedia + Journal both get search/filtering
Follow-up to the earlier QoL pass, using Alex's real answers instead of guessing.

1. Shift-sync root cause found for real. Queried Supabase directly rather than assume: the shifts genuinely ARE there (through July 26, written by Alex's local Python browser-use automation script — a real external write path, outside the app entirely, that the earlier investigation hadn't accounted for). The actual bug: rpgace_shifts reads went through RPGACE.cache's 60s TTL, and an external script's writes can never trigger this app's own cache-busting (which only ever fires from RPGACE's own insert/update/delete wrappers) — so any read could serve stale data with no way for the app to know. 🔓 July 22 — SECURITY: every /api/*.js endpoint has ZERO server-side auth — bigger exposure than the known RLS gap, nothing fixed yet
Surfaced while Aintergration-processing book batch 2's "how do we design safe APIs" chapter — checked directly rather than assumed. Read api/oracle.js and api/_context.js: setCORS() sets Access-Control-Allow-Origin: '*', and there is no token, header, or password check anywhere in any /api/*.js file. CORRECT_PW in main.js is a client-side JavaScript gate on the HTML page only — it never touches the API. Anyone who discovers rpgace.vercel.app/api/oracle or /api/orchestrate can call it directly with curl or from another website's fetch, completely bypassing the password screen, running up real Anthropic/Composio billing on Alex's own keys or triggering real Gmail/Notion/GitHub actions with no gate at all. This is a bigger real exposure than the already-known RLS-disabled-on-3-tables issue — that one risks app data, this one risks Alex's actual account billing and real third-party actions.

Not fixed this session — the minimal fix (a shared-secret header the client sends and every /api/*.js file checks) touches every API file plus needs `main.js`'s frozen-file deliberate-exception process to actually send that header from the client. Real decision, not a drive-by patch. Flagged plainly per the standing security-disclosure discipline (surface clearly, let Alex decide, never silently patch a security issue without confirmation) — Tier 3.

🪞 July 22 — Oracle gains self-awareness (Phase 1) + a suggestion bridge back to Claude Code (Phase 2); Phase 3 (autonomous action-triggering) explicitly parked
Alex asked for Oracle to become "the orchestrator for all modules, buttons, cards, dashboard etc" and to be able to know its own limitations/underused capabilities and suggest fixes back to a Claude Code session — a two-way bridge. GODMODE evidence pass first: Oracle had zero awareness of RPGACE's own dashboard or any of its 34 internal modules — the only existing "orchestrator" was /api/orchestrate.js's fixed 6-action Composio whitelist for EXTERNAL services only (Gmail/Notion/YouTube/Instagram/GitHub/Canva). Also confirmed: RPGACE_ORACLE_NOTES.md, the "Oracle writes suggestions, Claude Code reads them as suggestions never instructions" bridge CLAUDE.md already specs, was never built — and can't work exactly as originally written, since Oracle runs as a stateless Vercel function with no persistent or git-writable filesystem to edit a markdown file with.

Council of 5 verdict: split the ask into three. Read-only self-awareness is safe and cheap (same risk profile as oracleTreeGrounding, already shipped and proven). A suggestion-bridge is safe once the transport is corrected to something Oracle can actually write to. Autonomous action-triggering across internal modules is real risk — several of the 34 modules write or delete data, exactly why CLAUDE.md's human-checkpoint-on-every-taxonomy-write rule exists, and hostile fetched web content Oracle already ingests could try to abuse a generic "call anything" bridge. Asked Alex directly rather than defaulting; Alex confirmed: build Phases 1 and 2, do NOT build Phase 3 — park it as a future idea.

Phase 1 shipped — oracleAppGrounding module: same proven wrap-window.callOracle pattern as oracleTreeGrounding (own guard flag, composes safely with it regardless of init order). Cheap keyword gate — only injects when a message is actually capability/roadmap-shaped ("what can you do", "what's broken", "what should I build next", etc.) — then appends the live dashDeck.MODULES card list (zero duplication, reads the same array the UI renders from) plus a hand-maintained condensed honest-status digest (10/21 phyla live, F16-18 never hand-tested, the 504 bug, the 3 RLS-disabled tables, etc.) — explicitly told never to invent a feature or claim something's finished that hasn't been hand-tested. Costs nothing when it doesn't trigger.

Phase 2 shipped — oracleDevBridge module + new Supabase table: reuses the existing oracle:response-scanned hook (already fired once per completed Oracle reply for phylumPath's auto-detect badge) instead of a second MutationObserver on the chat box — the exact duplication CLAUDE.md's own landmines already flag as a bug found and fixed once. Adds a "🧪 Flag for Claude Code" button after every substantial Oracle reply; a manual click is the human checkpoint, nothing auto-writes. New table oracle_dev_suggestions (id/created_at/suggestion_text/category/status/source), created via a real migration after Alex confirmed the exact SQL — RLS enabled with the identical anon_all permissive policy every other RLS-enabled table in this project already uses (checked journal/encyclopedia's real policies first rather than guessing), so this table does NOT join the 3 pre-existing RLS-disabled ones. A future Claude Code session reads this table the same way Alex already queries Supabase directly — suggestions only, promoted into CLAUDE.md only with Alex's explicit confirmation, same boundary as the never-built file-based version.

node --check rpgace_core.js clean. Not yet hand-tested — needs a real click-through: ask Oracle a capability question and confirm the grounding block actually shows up in its answer; check a normal Oracle reply for the flag button and confirm a click actually inserts a row into oracle_dev_suggestions.

The real, buildable version of what Alex wanted: Oracle already has a proven pattern for exactly this — a dedicated command with its own prompt template, same shape as Prod Oracle's other 14 commands. Found the actual live mechanism first (GODMODE evidence pass, not assumed): the static 8-button #prod-oracle-panel in index.html is dead UI — prodOraclePanel (rpgace_core.js, module already shipped) intercepts toggleProdOraclePanel() and opens its own 14-command slide-out panel instead, each command running through RPGACE.utils.fillGaps() (step-by-step bracket-filling popup) → RPGACE.utils.sendToOracle().

Not yet hand-tested — needs a real click-through on rpgace.vercel.app (Prod.
