Roadmap — Reboot Style
Future Patch Notes — Full Step-by-Step Roadmap
Every confirmed-but-unbuilt item across this entire session (July 1–6), consolidated into one numbered, dependency-ordered checklist — same format as the original July 1st Reboot's 36-step plan. Each step has a domain, what it does, and its build command where applicable.

Tier 6 — Long-Range Vision Items (6a-6f), confirmed direction, not yet scoped for build (renamed Aug 11 — this heading used to say "Future Integrations," now a real, separate oversight artifact at future_integrations.html; same content, different name to avoid confusion)

6a — Social media platform integrations
Direct posting/scheduling integrations beyond current Composio connectors. Not yet scoped to specific platforms or build order — logged as confirmed direction.
6b — Video editing and scripting integration
Extends Steps F17 (Video Pipeline Tab) and F18 (Visual Treatment Generator) — connects RPGACE to actual editing/scripting tools, not just planning them.
6c — Full learning environment for music production + posting
A structured curriculum layer over the existing Feynman Loop + Encyclopedia + Taxonomy Tree — teaching production AND posting/growth as one connected learning path, not two separate systems.
6d — Auto-logging videos, beats, and career progress
Passive, automatic logging of creative output and career milestones — extends Journal + Content Production Live to capture progress without manual entry.
6e — Autonomous RPGACE self-improvement meta-agent NOVEL GOVERNANCE PATTERN — CONFIRMED, PRECISE SPEC
An AI agent that continuously monitors AI development and social media platform developments, comparing RPGACE's current structure against what a "best possible version" would look like with the latest relevant feature integrated.

Confirmed decision rule, stated precisely: if the agent identifies a genuinely good new idea not yet in RPGACE, it asks the user before adopting. If it identifies that something already integrated is a poor implementation, it does NOT ask — it acts directly (fixes or replaces it). This is an explicit two-tier autonomy rule: permission required for new adoption, no permission required for correcting confirmed-bad existing implementation.

This is the most architecturally unusual system logged all session — an agent with a standing mandate to modify RPGACE's own code based on its own judgment of quality, not just execute user-issued patches. Needs careful scoping (what counts as "confirmed shit," what stops it from over-correcting) before any build begins.
6f — Competitive/book/article learning pipeline into Taxonomy CONFIRMED, WITH WORKED EXAMPLE
The environment learns from competitor research and book/article drops the user provides. Best, most unique insights get extracted and pushed down the taxonomy tree, which then feeds the database, which then surfaces in Oracle responses and Encyclopedia entries as source material.

Worked example, given directly by the user — this should become the template for every Sorting-Agent-created leaf node:
A reverb technique using Valhalla (specific knobs, specific sound) gets extracted → logged as a taxonomy leaf node titled "Valhalla reverb VST used" → the leaf's summary is structured to give a complete outlook: what the VST is, how it works, how it's used, on what sounds.

Confirmed requirement: every such leaf node includes clickable footnotes linking back to the original source — this is precisely why the Content Intelligence bibliography system exists (intel_bibliography table), and confirms that table's purpose extends beyond Content Intelligence into taxonomy leaf sourcing generally.

Open question for next session: the exact structure of a "complete outlook" summary (what fields, what order, how long) needs to be designed once — a template — so every leaf node created this way is consistent, rather than each one being separately reasoned about from scratch.

STEP F8 — Formal phylum-selection criteria SHIPPED JULY 12
Replaced the blunt raw-count 2+ keyword threshold with a weighted scoring model, applied everywhere phylum matching happens. Concretely:

1. Full 21-phylum coverage — 7 phyla (11, 15, 17–21) had zero keywords and could never be silently matched by anything; all now have real keyword sets. 21 (Miscellaneous) deliberately kept empty — it's the catch-all bucket, giving it keywords would let the scanner match on it when it shouldn't.
2. Weighted terms — every keyword is now {term, weight}: 2 for terms specific/rare outside their phylum (sidechain, beatstars, 808), 1 for generic terms that could plausibly show up in several (style, tone). Threshold moved from raw count ≥2 to weighted score ≥3 (RPGACE.utils.PHYLA_MATCH_THRESHOLD), so two generic words alone can no longer trigger a match the way they used to.
3. Word-boundary matching — was naive text.includes(keyword), which false-positived on short terms inside unrelated words ("key" matching "monkey"/"turkey"/"keyword"). Now regex word-boundary matching.
4. Fixed a real overlap — 14 (Visio Cinematica) and the new 11 (Lingua Musicae) both claimed "colour" and "mood board". Split them: 14 keeps concrete filmmaking-production terms, 11 keeps abstract aesthetic/branding terms.
5. Found and consolidated a third independent copy of this same keyword list, in contentRepurpose._detectPhyla — missed by F2's consolidation, still on the old 14-phylum/raw-count model. Now reuses the same shared scorer instead of its own hardcoded duplicate, reusing taxonomyTree.PHYLUM_ENGLISH for its display text instead of a fourth hand-written description string.
6. Found (but left alone) a fourth, separate list — taxonomySync.PHYLUM_MAP, which feeds the older taxonomy_nodes table via a single-best-match classifier, a genuinely different job (bulk-classify into exactly one bucket vs multi-match confidence-gated scanning) — not merged into the new scorer, since that would change its behaviour beyond what was asked. Did fix one concrete bug in it: its zero-score fallback default was {num: 10, name: 'Technologia'}, but PHYLUM_MAP itself has 10 as Psychologia and 20 as Technologia — every unmatched entry landed with a self-contradictory num/name pair. Fixed to {num: 20, ...}. Worth a future pass to decide if PHYLUM_MAP should be retired in favour of the shared list, but that's a call about the older taxonomy_nodes table's continued purpose, not a quick fix.

July 14 follow-up: Phylum 1 (Compositio)'s keyword entry enriched from 7 terms to ~140 — a comprehensive music-theory/production jargon sweep (scales/modes, chord types, progressions, melody construction, harmony, intervals, UK-drill production slang, FL Studio-specific terms), deduplicated against the existing 7 and weighted using the same 2=specific/1=generic convention. This is deliberately asymmetric with every other phylum (still 6-8 terms each) — Phylum 1 is the pilot for the new "Phylum Path" concept (see the plan doc at /root/.claude/plans/woolly-watching-lamport.md), and this list is also its seed vocabulary. Worth knowing: since this scorer runs against all 21 phyla for the Oracle badge, contentRepurpose, and taxonomyReviewQueue's auto-propose gating, Phylum 1 will now win close calls more easily than before anywhere melody/harmony/chord language appears — an intentional tradeoff for the pilot, not rebalanced against the other 20 yet.

STEP F10 — n8n scheduler for rota automation (was: Windows Task Scheduler) WORKFLOW READY — NEEDS ONE MANUAL TEST RUN
Re-checked against scripts/fourth_rota.py's real source (July 12) before wiring a scheduled task, per this doc's own rule of not guessing twice — and the original premise here didn't hold. This entry said the script "already supports unattended runs (headed browser, credentials file, direct Supabase push) — only the OS-level trigger is missing." None of that was true as written:

1. No credentials file — username = input(...) and password = getpass.getpass(...) blocked on interactive terminal input every run, unconditionally.
2. Two more manual gates — if auto-login-detection or auto-nav-to-schedule fails, the script literally prints "please log in manually" / "please navigate to your schedule" and blocks on input("Press Enter once...").
3. No direct Supabase push — it writes a browser console command to rota_console_cmd.txt and prints instructions to manually paste it into DevTools on rpgace.vercel.app. There is no Supabase write in this file at all.
4. Browser launches headless=False always — under Task Scheduler with no interactive desktop session this would likely fail to even open a window.

Fixed today (#1 only): added get_credentials(), reading username/password from a local .fourth_credentials file (line 1 = username, line 2 = password) if present, falling back to the old interactive prompts otherwise. Gate #2 (the two "press Enter" fallbacks) and #3 (manual console paste instead of a real Supabase push) are deliberately untouched — #2 may exist because of MFA/anti-bot detection on Fourth's login, which would cap how far unattended automation can go regardless, and that needs verifying with a real run before more code gets written around it; #3 wasn't part of what was asked for this round. Registering the actual trigger is still premature until a real run confirms auto-login-detection reaches the schedule page without ever hitting gate #2 — if it does, the job will hang exactly as before, just one step further in.

Pivoted the trigger from Windows Task Scheduler to n8n, since there's already a self-hosted n8n instance running (confirmed via the "RPGACE n8n" GitHub OAuth app, authorized previously — unrelated to this automation, its scopes are far broader than anything here needs). Added n8n/rota_sync_workflow.json: a Cron trigger (Monday 8am) → Execute Command node running fourth_rota.py, importable via n8n's editor. No direct connection to the actual n8n instance was available this round (self-hosted, not exposed, and exposing it costs money that wasn't worth spending just for this) — so this is an importable file to review and activate manually, not something wired up live. n8n/README.md has the setup steps, including the same "run it once manually first" caution as above, and a flag that the Execute Command node's path assumes native (non-Docker) n8n on the same Windows machine as the repo checkout.

STEP F16 — Beatstars listing generator (orig. spec: auto-listing) SHIPPED JULY 13 — SCOPED DOWN
Premise-check first, like F10: confirmed via web search that BeatStars has no public API for creating listings — it's a repeatedly-requested, still-unimplemented feature on their end, not something RPGACE can call into. So F16 was rescoped from "auto-create" to "auto-generate the listing content, human does the final manual paste" — same pattern as F10's Fourth automation.

Added a "🎧 Beatstars Listing" button to contentProductionLive's ConID action row (only shown once licence_type is set on that ConID — i.e. it's actually a beat sale, per F15's fields). _generateBeatstarsListing(row) looks up a matching video_jobs row from Beat Log (status='beat_logged', fuzzy title match) to pull real BPM/key/scale/mood if that beat was logged first, falls back gracefully to ConID-only data if no match or the lookup fails, then sends Oracle a prompt covering the licence's real terms (lease/non-exclusive/exclusive text blocks written out, not left to Oracle to invent) and price. Oracle returns: 3 SEO-formatted title options, a 150-200 word description, 10-15 BeatStars tags, and the licence terms block — all ready to copy-paste directly into BeatStars' listing fields.

Correction, same day: when this shipped, the video_jobs lookup above would always have hit the graceful fallback — the table didn't exist in Supabase yet (found while scoping F17, see below). Table now created; the BPM/key/mood lookup should actually find matches going forward.
STEP F17 — Video Pipeline tracker (orig. spec: nav tab, EDL review, render) SHIPPED JULY 13 — SCOPED DOWN
Premise-check first, same as F10/F16: the original spec assumed a render/EDL job queue served by local_server.py. Real source-reading found local_server.py only exposes /reports, /push-to-supabase, /watchlist — Content Intelligence endpoints, nothing render-related — and no EDL generator or render engine exists anywhere else in the stack (video_jobs.edl was always written null; "Neural Frames" is an external tool the human pastes prompts into, RPGACE doesn't render into it). Rescoped, per direction, to a status tracker with no rendering happening in RPGACE.

Bigger finding while scoping this: the video_jobs table beatLog has been writing to since it shipped did not exist in Supabase at all — every Beat Log save has been silently failing (the insert's error is swallowed by a console-only .catch()). Created the table this session (migration create_video_jobs_table) with the columns beatLog already assumed (title, status, script, edl, raw_path, style_profile_id) plus what the tracker needed (edited_path, rendered_path, export_paths jsonb, notes, updated_at). Beat Log's writes should actually persist from now on — not verified by hand yet.

New videoPipeline module: dashboard widget mirroring contentProductionLive's ConID tracker exactly (same progress-dot bar, same badge/status pattern) over video_jobs rows. Stages: Beat Logged → Raw Footage → Edited → Rendered → Exported, one "→ Mark next stage" button per row. "📋 Paths + exports" popup holds per-stage file paths plus the 4 export slots (YouTube/Instagram/TikTok/Beatstars) the original spec asked for — plain text fields for a URL or local path once that step is done manually, no auto-population. "+ New" button creates a standalone video job (for videos that don't originate from Beat Log).
STEP F18 — Visual Treatment Generator (orig. Step 35) SHIPPED JULY 13
Optional checkbox on the Beat Log form ("🎬 Also generate Visual Treatment Doc") — off by default since it's a second Oracle call on top of the main beat-log prompt. When checked, beatLog._waitThenAutoVisualTreatment() polls window._oracleRequestInFlight (the concurrency guard from today's earlier fix) until the main beat-log Oracle response clears, then _autoVisualTreatment() fires visualOracle's own Visual Treatment Doc structure automatically with the beat's real title/key/scale/BPM/mood/colour-palette already filled in — no manual trip through the Visual Oracle panel, no placeholder-filling. Grounded in the real F14 filmmaker library the same way Director Match is (reuses visualOracle._withFilmmakerLibrary()), so Oracle picks and names a real director rather than inventing one.

Claude Code fallback lane — real, deployed, but genuinely UNVERIFIED behavior JULY 24 — NEEDS A REAL CREDIT-EXHAUSTION EVENT TO PROVE
Deployment itself is confirmed real, not assumed: Vercel shows commit 5887545 READY on production, and /api/data-write still returns a genuine 401 unauthenticated (the write-proxy's own gate is intact). What's genuinely unverified is the FEATURE'S behavior, not its presence in production:

1. The credit-exhaustion detector has never seen a real failure. phylumPath._isCreditExhaustionError's string match ("credit balance" / "insufficient credit") is a best guess at Anthropic's real error wording, checked only against the code that propagates it, never against a live failure. Fails open by design — a wrong guess means the feature silently never triggers, never a crash — but this means nobody knows yet whether it actually catches the real thing.

2. A parse failure on a fallback answer leaves a row silently stuck, retried every 5 minutes by _checkFallbackAnswers, failing the same way each time with only a console.warn — no toast, nothing Alex would notice without opening devtools or querying Supabase directly.

3. A real future landmine, written into CLAUDE.md's landmines section: oracle_fallback_queue must never be folded into a future Approach B RLS-restriction batch without also giving the Fallback Drain Routine (which writes back via the plain anon key, no service-role access) a way to still write to it — doing so would silently break the whole lane with no visible error.

4. Bookworm's auto-resume only covers a cascade's FIRST placement attempt per phylum — a failure during a reword-retry or upgrade-check sub-call isn't queued/resumed, by design, not a bug.

None of these are reasons the build is wrong — they're exactly what "not yet hand-tested against a genuine credit-exhaustion event" honestly means. First real signal: whenever Alex's API credits actually run low, check oracle_fallback_queue and the Routine's own run history, don't assume it worked.

XSS / innerHTML audit of fetched external content — still not started RESOLVED JULY 28 — kept here as a record
Flagged July 22 (Aintergration book batch 3): oracleFetchGuard hardens Oracle against fetched content manipulating the MODEL, but does nothing for fetched content injecting raw HTML/script into the PAGE via innerHTML. Whether any real call site renders un-escaped fetched-external text (Jina page text, YouTube transcripts, Oracle's own markdown reply) was never checked. Deferred alongside the 504 fix — same credits constraint, and the two pair naturally as one Oracle-hardening pass next session.

Resolved July 28; corrected here July 31. The audit ran and found a real bug: main.js's renderMarkdown() — behind every Oracle chat message — injected raw response text into innerHTML with zero HTML-escaping, so fetched web/YouTube/Instagram content Oracle quoted verbatim (or a successful prompt injection) could render live markup in the chat DOM. Fixed via a new _escChatHtml() step before the markdown regex chain; markdown still renders correctly and a literal script tag now comes through inert. FROZEN-file exception logged.

Architecture fragility — ~25 places in rpgace_core.js may share the rpgace:ready re-subscription bug CLOSED JULY 29 — kept here as a record
Found while diagnosing why the Phase 2 nav tab wasn't appearing (see the July 16 card above): RPGACE.register() wires every module's init() as a listener on 'rpgace:ready' itself, so init() only ever runs because that event already fired — but RPGACE.hooks.fire()'s plain Array.forEach never revisits listeners pushed onto the array during its own iteration. Any module that calls RPGACE.hooks.on('rpgace:ready', ...) from inside its own init() is registering a listener too late for the pass currently invoking it — silently, no error, forever. phylumPath's 2 occurrences (side-panel button, nav tab) were fixed. Grep found ~25 more occurrences of the same call in rpgace_core.js beyond R.register's own definition — each needs checking individually: called from inside that same module's own init() (the buggy pattern) versus genuinely reacting to another module's readiness from elsewhere (fine). Not audited this pass.

Closed July 29, re-confirmed by grep July 31. A real re-audit found these sites had been fixed incidentally over time as modules were rewritten. Exactly one hooks.on('rpgace:ready', ...) call remains in rpgace_core.js, and it is RPGACE.register()'s own canonical module-init machinery — not a bug site. The underlying hooks.fire() behaviour is still true and still recorded in CLAUDE.md's landmines; there is simply no backlog attached to it any more.

Meta
How This Manual Works
Purpose
This is a living, dated log — not a replacement for the V6 architecture manual. Each session that ships fixes or features gets its own dated entry, appended below the most recent one, newest at top. It exists specifically to prevent re-diagnosing the same bug twice and to keep an honest record of what was tried and failed, not just what eventually worked.

Format for future entries: Summary → Shipped → Regression/Incident log (if any, kept in chronological order including wrong turns) → Root cause detail (for anything that took more than 2 exchanges to diagnose) → Lessons logged → Still open. Every entry gets a date-tag and its own anchor in the sidebar nav.
